Two Hundred ($200.00) Dollars; for each subsequent offense, shall be fined as for the first, and in addition thereto, confined in the county jail for not less than one month and not more than three months, and in either event shall pay the cost of prosecution.

RECIPROCAL LICENSURE SEC. 6. Reciprocal licensure shall be extended to Chiro practors licensed and practicing in such States as granted this courtesy to Chiropractors licensed and practicing in the State of Oklahoma.

REPEALING CLAUSE SEC. 7. All Acts and parts of Acts in conflict with this Act, are hereby repealed. 

Approved February 21, 1921.

RULES AND REGULATIONS OF THE BOARD of CHIROPRACTIC EXAMINERS 

State of Oklahoma

ADOPTED JULY 9TH, 1921 REVISED AND AMENDED MARCH 20, 1922 

1. The Board will adhere to the provisions of Section 

8898 to 8903 inclusive, Compiled Oklahoma Statutes 1921. 

2. These rules and regulations are subject to change or revision at any regular or special meeting of the Board. 

3. All proceedings of the Board shall be governed by 

Robert's Rules of Order. [369]

4. The Board shall approve or disapprove the bond of 

Treasurer, given under the Act. The Secretary shall be the custodian of said Bond for the Board. 

5. All examinations shall be conducted in the English language. Applicants will be examined in writing on the subjects prescribed by the Act, as taught by Chiropractic schools or colleges. The applicant will be examined in palpating, nerve tracing, and adjusting in oral portion of the examination prescribed by the Act in such manner and to such extent as will satisfy the Board of his admissibility. 

Time, semi-annually, beginning at 9 A.M., the second 

Monday of July and January, of each year, at the Capitol. 

6. A photograph of each applicant must accompany the application, and same must have been taken within one year prior to submission of the application. All applications for examination or reciprocal licensure shall be made on blanks furnished by the Board. On all regular examinations, application must be in the hands of the Secretary at least 15 days prior to date of examination. 

7. Upon request for application blank the Secretary will mail applicant copy of the Rules and Regulations. 

8. It shall be the object of this Board to foster higher professional standards as rapidly as is consistent with the best interests of the profession. In this, it shall not be swayed or influenced by any school interests whatsoever. 

9. Applicants will subscribe to the pledge contained in the application blank furnished by the Board, and promise to report to this Board all infractions of the law and of the rulings of this Board by other practitioners. They will further pledge themselves to assist in enforcing the law in every manner possible, as it is obvious that a Board of three members cannot be expected to secure all the information necessary to keep the State free from imposters. 

10. Whenever the Board shall deem it expedient, it may cause to appear before it any applicant whose right to be licensed may not be clearly defined; or any other person making affidavit regarding an applicant when desired by the [370]

APPENDIX 

Board. It may also in its discretion, refuse such applicant license forthwith. 

11. Successful applicants for license must at all times display conspicuously, in their offices, the license granted them by this Board. 

12. Constructive educational publicity is encouraged. 

Applicants must promise to refrain from using or causing to be used, advertising matter which contains mis-statements, falsehoods, misrepresentations, distorted or fabulous statements as to cures, etc. 13. 

No credit whatever will be allowed on the educational requirements of the Act for any so-called home extension, or Correspondence study. 

14. Any false statement or certificate furnished by any applicant to the Board on any material fact required by the laws of Oklahoma or the rules of this Board shall be deemed by the Board a sufficient warrant for the rejection of the application for a license to practice the science of Chiropractic. 

15. All credentials written in any language other than

English shall be translated into the language and certified to under the seal of the Consulate. 

16. All Chiropractors licensed under this Act shall keep the Board notified at all times of any change in their mailing address. 

17. This Board does not issue temporary licenses to practice in Oklahoma. The filing of application to the Board, therefore, does not grant any special privileges. 

18. Any license issued by this Board may be revoked for failure on the part of the holder thereof to comply with the laws of Oklahoma and the rules and regulations promulgated by this Board, or for any conduct involving moral turpitude; and the acceptance by an applicant of a license by the Board hereunder shall be with the express and distinct understanding on the part of such licensee that the license is held by him subject to this restriction. 

19. Reciprocity will be granted only to those states which extend like recognition to Oklahoma, and who through [371]

their Boards fill out the prescribed blanks and return to this Board duly certified. We give and require six months' notice of intention to withdraw from reciprocity with any state. 

The fee for reciprocity will be $50.00, the same to accompany application. Application blanks will be furnished by the Secretary of the Board to reciprocity applicants on request. 

20. The Board will engage counsel, at an annual retainer, for the purpose of advising the Board on all matters of procedure and law which may arise, such retainer to be paid as a part of the expenses of the Board, from such funds as may be realized from application fees, and to engage such clerical help as may be found necessary and expedient in the conduct of its affairs. 

21. Chiropractors filling out affidavits as to "contents of application" must be legally qualified practitioners of Chiropractic in their respective states. Those filling out character affidavits must be legally qualified and holders of unrevoked licenses to practice in Oklahoma. 

ADDITIONAL RULES ADOPTED JULY 10, 1922 

22. This Board will not admit to examination nor license any applicant who has practiced Chiropractic in this State prior to examination by this Board, or who has maintained an office for the practice of any method wherein disrelated tissue was related, unless he was the possessor of a license issued by some other duly constituted Oklahoma State 

Board so to do. 

23. That provision of the law requiring a three-year course in Chiropractic Schools or Colleges of not less than six months each is not deemed complied with by simply attending classes in two colleges at the same time. 

ADDITIONAL RULE ADOPTED JULY 23, 1923 

24. To more completely serve the Chiropractic profession in Oklahoma and to enable the intelligent recommendation as to character, etc., on the part of the Board and to promote harmony among the several state Boards of Exam- [372]

APPENDIX 

iners, the Board of Chiropractic Examiners, State of Oklahoma, will, from and after July 23, 1923, expect from Chiropractors practicing in the State of Oklahoma, who desire reciprocal licensure with other states, a certificate of good moral character, certifying that the applicant has conducted himself in an honorable, ethical and upright manner, from the President of the Oklahoma State Association of Licensed Chiropractors. Upon the receipt of this certificate, executed in form, the Board of Examiners will recommend such applicants for reciprocal licensure with the several state Boards who recognize Oklahoma licensees for reciprocal purposes. 

ADDITIONAL RULE ADOPTED JULY 14, 1924 

25. No school or college of Chiropractic will be considered reputable which matriculates students for a so-called 

Home Extension, Correspondence, Night or Evening Course.
